\displaystyle\displaystyle-2\lambda^{2}m^{6}\sinh^{3}\theta-i\frac{\lambda^{2}m^{6}}{4800\pi}\cosh^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\bigg[27683+12840\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-19200\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}

\displaystyle\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\displaystyle 2\cosh\theta\big(10447+5940\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-19200\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\big)\bigg]\ .

Therefore, by (
35
) the second order
\displaystyle S
-matrix is,

\displaystyle\displaystyle S^{(2)}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{A}^{(2)}}{4m^{2}\sinh\theta}

(202)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{2}m^{4}\sinh^{2}\theta-i\frac{\lambda^{2}m^{4}}{38400\pi}\coth\frac{\theta}{2}\bigg[27683+12840\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-19200\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}

\displaystyle\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\displaystyle 2\cosh\theta\big(10447+5940\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-19200\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\big)\bigg]\ .

It is interesting to note that at second order, the real part of the amplitude comes only from the
\displaystyle s
-channel, while the
\displaystyle t
and
\displaystyle u
-channels give purely imaginary contributions to the amplitude.

4.2
Renormalized Lagrangian

Now that the
\displaystyle S
-matrix has been computed the
\displaystyle T\bar{T}
-deformed theory can be renormalized by demanding that the
\displaystyle S
-matrix has the form (
7
).

Discarding the imaginary finite pieces of second order
\displaystyle S
-matrix (
202
) yields,
10
10

10

The finite pieces are not essential as one can change them by rescaling the cut-off, their choices are just equivalent to using different regularization schemes.

\displaystyle\displaystyle S^{(2)}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{2}m^{4}\sinh^{2}\theta-i\frac{\lambda^{2}m^{4}}{38400\pi}\coth\frac{\theta}{2}\bigg[12840\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-19200\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}-2\cosh\theta\big(5940\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\displaystyle 19200\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{2}m^{4}\sinh^{2}\theta-i\frac{\lambda^{2}m^{4}}{\pi}\coth\frac{\theta}{2}\bigg[\frac{107-99\cosh\theta}{320}\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-\frac{1-2\cosh\theta}{2}\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\bigg]\ .

Observe how the first term in (
) is exactly the expected second order
\displaystyle S
-matrix in an integrable field theory, but (
) also contains an extra divergent imaginary part.
The
\displaystyle S
-matrix was computed using the classical (bare) Lagrangian (
29
), so counterterms must be added to the classical Lagrangian in order to get rid of the imaginary part of
\displaystyle S^{(2)}(\theta)
. In this process the Lagrangian will be perturbatively renormalized up to second order in the
\displaystyle T\bar{T}
-coupling
\displaystyle\lambda
.

It is important to mention that the integrable structure of the
\displaystyle T\bar{T}
-deformed free massive Dirac fermion theory is what enables the theory to be renormalized perturbatively. In general, the scattering amplitude of a quantum field theory may contain logarithms of functions of Mandelstam variables, which involves
\displaystyle\theta
11
11

11

(
) is an expression for the logarithm of a function of Mandelstam variables which appear while computing the one-loop momentum integrals in the scattering amplitude. When expressed in terms of rapidity, it yields terms involving
\displaystyle\theta
.
. However, local counterterms can never cancel a term involving
\displaystyle\theta
and can only cancel terms involving powers of
\displaystyle\cosh^{2}\Big(\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)
12
12

12

A polynomial in Mandelstam variable
\displaystyle s
corresponds to a polynomial in
\displaystyle\cosh^{2}\Big(\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)
, when expressed in terms of the rapidity.
. Due to the integrable structure of the theory, our final amplitude does not contain terms involving
\displaystyle\theta
although individual contributions to the amplitude have terms involving
\displaystyle\theta
. This is expected because the
\displaystyle S
-matrix of an integrable theory can not have branch cuts and can only have poles.

Discarding the imaginary finite pieces of the second order amplitude (
201
) yields,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-2\lambda^{2}m^{6}\sinh^{3}\theta-i\frac{8\lambda^{2}m^{6}}{\pi}\cosh^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\bigg[\frac{107-99\cosh\theta}{320}\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-\frac{1-2\cosh\theta}{2}\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\bigg]

(204)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-2\lambda^{2}m^{6}\sinh^{3}\theta-i\frac{8\lambda^{2}m^{6}}{\pi}\bigg[\Big(\frac{103}{160}\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-\frac{3}{2}\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\Big)\cosh^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\Big(-\frac{99}{160}\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle 2\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\Big)\cosh^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}\bigg]\ .

Let the renormalized Lagrangian be,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{ren}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-i\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}{\partial}_{\mu}\psi+m\bar{\psi}\psi+\frac{\lambda}{2}\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big)+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}m\bar{\psi}\psi\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{7}{4}\lambda^{2}m^{2}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}\ \tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}-\frac{7}{4}\lambda^{2}m^{3}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{3}+\alpha\lambda^{2}(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}+\beta\lambda^{2}\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{3})\ ,

where
\displaystyle\alpha
and
\displaystyle\beta
are divergent coefficients which must be tuned to exactly cancel the imaginary divergent contributions to the second order
\displaystyle S
-matrix (
).

The term
\displaystyle\alpha\lambda^{2}(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}
contributes to the amplitude as,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}^{\text{count}}=(i\alpha\lambda^{2})4m^{4}(1+\cosh\theta)=8i\alpha\lambda^{2}m^{4}\cosh^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\ .

(206)

While the term
\displaystyle\beta\lambda^{2}\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}
contributes to the amplitude as,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\text{count}}=(i\beta\lambda^{2})4m^{4}(\cosh\theta+\cosh 2\theta)=-8i\beta\lambda^{2}m^{4}\big(3\cosh^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-4\cosh^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}\big)\ .

(207)

Adding (
206
) and (
207
), gives the total contribution from the counterterms to the amplitude,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\text{count}}=\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}^{\text{count}}+\mathcal{A}_{\beta}^{\text{count}}=8i\lambda^{2}m^{4}\big(\alpha-3\beta\big)\cosh^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+32i\beta\lambda^{2}m^{4}\cosh^{4}\frac{\theta}{2}\ .

(208)

The condition that the sum amplitude of the counterterms, (
208
), must exactly cancel the imaginary part of (
204
) gives the conditions,

\displaystyle\displaystyle m^{4}\big(\alpha-3\beta\big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{m^{6}}{\pi}\Big(\frac{103}{160}\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-\frac{3}{2}\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\Big)\ ,

(209)

\displaystyle\displaystyle 32\beta m^{4}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{8m^{6}}{\pi}\Big(-\frac{99}{160}\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}+2\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\Big)\ .

(210)

Solving (
209
) and (
210
) for
\displaystyle\alpha
and
\displaystyle\beta
gives,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{23}{128\pi}\Lambda^{2}\ ,

(211)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}}{4\pi}\Big(-\frac{99}{160}\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}+2\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\Big).

(212)

Substituting the above values of
\displaystyle\alpha
and
\displaystyle\beta
into the renormalized Lagrangian (
) yields,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{ren}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-i\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}{\partial}_{\mu}\psi+m\bar{\psi}\psi+\frac{\lambda}{2}\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big)+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}m\bar{\psi}\psi\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big)

(213)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{7}{4}\lambda^{2}m^{2}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}\ \tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}-\frac{7}{4}\lambda^{2}m^{3}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{3}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{23\Lambda^{2}}{128\pi}\lambda^{2}(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}+\frac{m^{2}}{4\pi}\Big(-\frac{99}{160}\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}+2\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\Big)\lambda^{2}\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{3})\ .

However, the above renormalized Lagrangian is written in terms of the redefined fields. The renormalized Lagrangian can be written in terms of the original fields as,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{ren}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-i\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}{\partial}_{\mu}\psi+m\bar{\psi}\psi+\frac{\lambda}{2}\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}+2m\bar{\psi}\psi\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}-2m^{2}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}\Big)

(214)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}m\bar{\psi}\psi\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{23\Lambda^{2}}{128\pi}\lambda^{2}(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}+\frac{m^{2}}{4\pi}\Big(-\frac{99}{160}\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}+2\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\Big)\lambda^{2}\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{3})\ .

Finally, the renormalized Lagrangian of the
\displaystyle T\bar{T}
-deformed free massive Dirac fermion in two dimensional Euclidean spacetime is given by,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{ren}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle i\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}{\partial}_{\mu}\psi-m\bar{\psi}\psi-\frac{\lambda}{2}\Big(2m\bar{\psi}\psi\ \tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}-2m^{2}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}\Big)-\frac{g}{2}\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}+\frac{h}{2}(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}

(215)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}m\bar{\psi}\psi\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{3})\ ,

where the renormalized couplings are given by,

\displaystyle\displaystyle g

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lambda-\frac{\lambda^{2}m^{2}}{2\pi}\Big(\frac{99}{160}\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-2\log\frac{m}{\Lambda}\Big)\ ,

\displaystyle\displaystyle h

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lambda-\frac{23\lambda^{2}}{64\pi}\Lambda^{2}\ .

(216)

It is important to notice the major qualitative difference between the renormalized Lagrangian (
215
) and the classical Lagrangian (
29
). The classical Lagrangian (
29
) has only one scale,
\displaystyle\lambda
, which appears to be the coupling for all the quartic terms. However, the renormalized Lagrangian contains three different couplings,
\displaystyle\lambda
,
\displaystyle g
and
\displaystyle h
. In the renormalized Lagrangian, the two quartic terms
\displaystyle\bar{\psi}\psi\ \tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}
and
\displaystyle(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}
share the old classical coupling
\displaystyle\lambda
, where as the other two quartic terms
\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}
and
\displaystyle(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}
have very different couplings
\displaystyle g
and
\displaystyle h
, respectively.

5
Discussion

In this paper the
\displaystyle T\bar{T}
-deformed free massive Dirac fermion in two dimensions was studied. First, the Lagrangian of the deformed theory was stated and massaged into an easier form for amplitude calculations using a field redefinition. The two-to-two
\displaystyle S
-matrix of the fermion anti-fermion scattering process was computed to second order in the
\displaystyle T\bar{T}
-coupling
\displaystyle\lambda
. At first order, the
\displaystyle S
-matrix exactly matches the expected result for an integrable field theory (
7
). However, at second order the
\displaystyle S
-matrix matches the expected result up to some divergent imaginary second order terms. Counterterms were added to the Lagrangian to cancel these divergent pieces and ensure that the final second order
\displaystyle S
-matrix agrees with the expected result (
7
), in the process the renormalized Lagrangian was obtained. Amazingly, integrability allows the naively non-renormalizable theory to be renormalized perturbatively. The renormalized Lagrangian was qualitatively very different from the classical Lagrangian as there are three different coupling constants (
\displaystyle\lambda
,
\displaystyle g
and
\displaystyle h
) in the renormalized Lagrangian, while in the classical case there is only one coupling constant (
\displaystyle\lambda
). Thus, the quantum integrability here leads to a more complicated renormalized Lagrangian than the classical one. This is not what always happens in an integrable theory, for example, the integrable
\displaystyle\sinh
-Gordon model gives rise to a renormalized Lagrangian which has the same functional form as the classical one
Rosenhaus
. Further, the existence of the renormalized Lagrangian means that all quantities of the theory may now be computed using the standard QFT techniques. For example, one can compute the correlation functions of local fields perturbatively using the renormalized Lagrangian.

In this paper, renormalization was performed by computing the two-to-two
\displaystyle S
-matrix for the fermion anti-fermion scattering:
\displaystyle f_{1}+\bar{f}_{2}\rightarrow f_{3}+\bar{f}_{4}
, and adding counterterms to cancel the divergences. However, one may consider the other possible two-to-two scattering process in this theory, namely, the fermion-fermion scattering:
\displaystyle f_{1}+f_{2}\rightarrow f_{3}+f_{4}
. If this process had been chosen the same renormalized Lagrangian would be expected. The calculation of the
\displaystyle S
-matrix would involve an almost identical computation to what was done here except that only one plane wave solution to the Dirac equation,
\displaystyle u(k)
, would be present in the expressions.

The form of the renormalized Lagrangian is already exciting at second order in the
\displaystyle T\bar{T}
-coupling. It would be interesting to see how the renormalized Lagrangian looks at higher orders, because of the simple structure of the
\displaystyle S
-matrix the renormalized Lagrangian may have a simple and compact form. Similar to
Rosenhaus
, the second order real contribution to the
\displaystyle S
-matrix came from the
\displaystyle s
-channel only. It would be useful to understand why this occurs and whether this is a general property of the
\displaystyle T\bar{T}
-deformed integrable theories.

Appendices

Appendix A
Field redefinitions

The Lagrangian of the
\displaystyle T\bar{T}
-deformed free massive Dirac fermion in two dimensional Euclidean spacetime is given by (see (
29
)),

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\big(i\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}{\partial}_{\mu}\psi-m\bar{\psi}\psi\big)-\frac{\lambda}{2}\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}+2m\bar{\psi}\psi\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}-2m^{2}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}\Big)

(217)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}m\bar{\psi}\psi\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big).

One can perform the following field redefinitions to the Lagrangian,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\psi_{a}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\displaystyle\psi_{a}+\alpha\lambda\psi_{a}(\bar{\psi}\psi)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bar{\psi}_{a}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bar{\psi}_{a}+\alpha\lambda(\bar{\psi}\psi)\bar{\psi}_{a}\ ,

(218)

where ‘
\displaystyle\alpha
’ is assumed to be a real constant with dimension
\displaystyle 1
.

Under the field redefinitions the following terms transform as

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bar{\psi}\psi

\displaystyle\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\displaystyle B\bar{\psi}\psi

\displaystyle\displaystyle\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}{\partial}_{\mu}\psi

\displaystyle\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\displaystyle B\ \bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}{\partial}_{\mu}\psi+\alpha\lambda(1+\alpha\lambda\bar{\psi}\psi)\ \bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}\psi{\partial}_{\mu}(\bar{\psi}\psi)

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\partial}_{\mu}\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}\psi

\displaystyle\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\displaystyle B\ {\partial}_{\mu}\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}\psi+\alpha\lambda(1+\alpha\lambda\bar{\psi}\psi)\ \bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}\psi{\partial}_{\mu}(\bar{\psi}\psi)\ ,

(219)

where

\displaystyle\displaystyle B=1+2\alpha\lambda\bar{\psi}\psi+\alpha^{2}\lambda^{2}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}.

(220)

By (
27
) and (
219
),
\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}
and
\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}
transform as,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\rightarrow B\ \tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}\rightarrow B\ \tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}.

(221)

Substituting the above redefined fields into the original Lagrangian (
217
) one obtains the redefined Lagrangian,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\lambda)=\mathcal{L}_{0}^{\prime}(\lambda)+\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}(\lambda)+\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\prime}(\lambda)

(222)

where,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}^{\prime}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\big(i\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}{\partial}_{\mu}\psi-m\bar{\psi}\psi\big)+2\alpha\lambda\bar{\psi}\psi\big[\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}-m\bar{\psi}\psi\big]+\alpha^{2}\lambda^{2}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}\big[\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}-m\bar{\psi}\psi\big]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2}\big[(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}-\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}\big]-\lambda m\bar{\psi}\psi\big[\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}-m\bar{\psi}\psi\big]+2\alpha\lambda^{2}\bar{\psi}\psi\big[(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}-\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\displaystyle 2m\bar{\psi}\psi\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}+2m^{2}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\prime}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}m\bar{\psi}\psi\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big)\ .

(223)

In order to simplify the computation of the
\displaystyle S
-matrix contributions from the bubble diagrams it is useful to get rid of the linear terms
\displaystyle\bar{\psi}\psi\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}
and
\displaystyle(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}
from the new redefined Lagrangian (
222
). This can be done by choosing
\displaystyle\alpha=\frac{m}{2}
. Substituting
\displaystyle\alpha=\frac{m}{2}
into the new redefined Lagrangian (
223
) gives,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\big(i\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}{\partial}_{\mu}\psi-m\bar{\psi}\psi\big)-\frac{\lambda}{2}\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big)

(224)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}m\bar{\psi}\psi\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big)-\frac{7}{4}\lambda^{2}m^{2}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}+\frac{7}{4}\lambda^{2}m^{3}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{3}

Therefore, the Lorentzian action in terms of the redefined fields can be written as

\displaystyle\displaystyle-I=i\int dtdx\,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\Big[

\displaystyle\displaystyle\big(-i\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}{\partial}_{\mu}\psi+m\bar{\psi}\psi\big)+\frac{\lambda}{2}\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}m\bar{\psi}\psi\Big(\tilde{X}_{\mu\nu}\tilde{X}^{\mu\nu}-(\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu})^{2}\Big)+\frac{7}{4}\lambda^{2}m^{2}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{2}\tilde{X}_{\mu}^{\ \mu}-\frac{7}{4}\lambda^{2}m^{3}(\bar{\psi}\psi)^{3}\Big]

Appendix B
Propagators and some useful formula

In this appendix the values of all the propagators that will arise in the
\displaystyle T\bar{T}
-deformed theory will be derived.
In two dimensions, the propagator of the free massive Dirac fermion is given by,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\langle\psi_{a}(x)\bar{\psi}_{b}(y)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{i(\gamma\cdot q+m)_{ab}}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}e^{-iq(x-y)}=\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}G_{ab}(q)e^{-iq(x-y)}\ ,

(226)

where,

\displaystyle\displaystyle G_{ab}(q)=\frac{i(\gamma\cdot q+m)_{ab}}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}.

(227)

The value of the propagator needed in the computation of the tadpole diagrams can be computed as,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\langle\psi_{a}(x)\bar{\psi}_{b}(x)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{i(\gamma\cdot q+m)_{ab}}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}

(228)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle i\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\bigg[\frac{\gamma_{ab}\cdot q}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}+\frac{m\delta_{ab}}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}\bigg].

By Lorentz symmetry the first term in (
228
) vanishes, thus

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\langle\psi_{a}(x)\bar{\psi}_{b}(x)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle im\delta_{ab}\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{1}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}

(229)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle im\delta_{ab}(-i)\int_{0}^{\Lambda}\frac{d^{2}q_{E}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{1}{q_{E}^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{m}{4\pi}\log\big(\frac{\Lambda^{2}+m^{2}}{m^{2}}\big)\delta_{ab}\approx-\frac{m}{4\pi}\log\big(\frac{m^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\big)\delta_{ab}=N_{0}\ \delta_{ab}\ ,

where
\displaystyle N_{0}=-\frac{m}{4\pi}\log\big(\frac{m^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\big)
, in the second equality a Wick rotation to Euclidean signature was performed
13
13

13

\displaystyle q^{0}=iq_{E}^{0}
,
\displaystyle q^{2}=-q_{E}^{2}
and
\displaystyle d^{2}q\rightarrow id^{2}q_{E}
.
and the integral was regulated by a hard cutoff
\displaystyle\Lambda\ (\Lambda\gg m)
.

From (
226
) the values of propagators with derivatives can be derived. When there is a derivative acting on
\displaystyle\psi
one finds,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\langle{\partial}^{\mu}\psi_{a}(x)\bar{\psi}_{b}(y)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}x_{\mu}}\left\langle\psi_{a}(x)\bar{\psi}_{b}(y)\right\rangle=-i\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{i(\gamma\cdot q+m)_{ab}}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}\ q^{\mu}e^{-iq(x-y)}.

(230)

Hence,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\langle{\partial}^{\mu}\psi_{a}(x)\bar{\psi}_{b}(x)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\bigg[\frac{q^{\mu}(\gamma_{ab}\cdot q)}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}+\frac{m\delta_{ab}q^{\mu}}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}\bigg].

(231)

By Lorentz symmetry the second term vanishes yielding,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\langle{\partial}^{\mu}\psi_{a}(x)\bar{\psi}_{b}(x)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q^{\mu}q^{\nu}(\gamma_{ab})_{\nu}}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}=\frac{1}{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}(\gamma_{ab})_{\nu}\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q^{2}}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}.

(232)

As before, perform the integral (
232
) using a Wick rotation,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\langle{\partial}^{\mu}\psi_{a}(x)\bar{\psi}_{b}(x)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{i\Lambda^{2}}{8\pi}\Big[1+\frac{m^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\log\Big(\frac{m^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\Big)\Big]\gamma^{\nu}_{ab}=N_{1}\gamma^{\nu}_{ab}\ ,

(233)

where
\displaystyle N_{1}=\frac{i\Lambda^{2}}{8\pi}\Big[1+\frac{m^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\log\Big(\frac{m^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\Big)\Big]
.

Similarly when a derivative acts of
\displaystyle\bar{\psi}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\langle\psi_{a}(x){\partial}^{\mu}\bar{\psi}_{b}(x)\right\rangle=-\left\langle{\partial}^{\mu}\psi_{a}(x)\bar{\psi}_{b}(x)\right\rangle=-N_{1}\delta_{ab}.

(234)

If one derivative acts on
\displaystyle\psi
and a second on
\displaystyle\bar{\psi}
one finds,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\langle{\partial}^{\mu}\psi_{a}(x){\partial}^{\nu}\bar{\psi}_{b}(y)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}x_{\mu}}\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}y_{\nu}}\left\langle\psi_{a}(x)\bar{\psi}_{b}(y)\right\rangle=i\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{(\gamma\cdot q+m)_{ab}}{q^{2}-m^{2}+i\epsilon}\ q^{\mu}q^{\nu}e^{-iq(x-y)}.

Implementing Lorentz symmetry and performing a Wick rotation yields,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\left\langle{\partial}^{\mu}\psi_{a}(x){\partial}^{\nu}\bar{\psi}_{b}(x)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{m\Lambda^{2}}{8\pi}\Big(1+\frac{m^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\log\big(\frac{m^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}}\big)\Big)\eta^{\mu\nu}\delta_{ab}=imN_{1}\eta^{\mu\nu}\delta_{ab}.

(236)

Appendix C
One-loop integrals

In this section the values of the one-loop integrals that arise in the computation of the amplitude from the bubble diagrams will be derived.

Begin with the simplest integral,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{1}{(q^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}}=i\int\frac{d^{2}q_{E}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{1}{(q_{E}^{2}+\alpha^{2})^{2}}=\frac{i}{4\pi\alpha^{2}}~,

(237)

where a Wick rotation was performed and the integral was converted to polar coordinates.

Next evaluate the following divergent integral which will be used in the computation of future integrals,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q^{2}}{(q^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}}=-\frac{i}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\Lambda}dq_{E}\frac{q_{E}^{3}}{(q_{E}^{2}+\alpha^{2})^{2}}=\frac{i}{4\pi}\bigg[\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}+\alpha^{2}}-\log\Big(\frac{\Lambda^{2}+\alpha^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)\bigg]

(238)

where a Wick rotation was performed, the integral was converted to polar coordinates and the integral was regulated by a hard cut-off
\displaystyle\Lambda\ (\Lambda^{2}>>\alpha^{2})
.

The following divergent integrals can be evaluated using the Lorentz symmetry and (
238
),

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}}{(q^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}}=\frac{\eta_{\mu\nu}}{2}\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q^{2}}{(q^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}}=\frac{i}{8\pi}\eta_{\mu\nu}\bigg[\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{\Lambda^{2}+\alpha^{2}}-\log\Big(\frac{\Lambda^{2}+\alpha^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)\bigg]

(239)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}q_{\rho}q_{\lambda}}{(q^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\big(\eta_{\mu\nu}\eta_{\rho\lambda}+\eta_{\mu\rho}\eta_{\nu\lambda}+\eta_{\mu\lambda}\eta_{\nu\rho}\big)\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{(q^{2})^{2}}{(q^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}}

(240)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{i}{16\pi}C_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda}\bigg[\frac{\Lambda^{2}(\Lambda^{2}+2\alpha^{2})}{2(\Lambda^{2}+\alpha^{2})}-\alpha^{2}\log\Big(\frac{\Lambda^{2}+\alpha^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)\bigg]\ ,

where,

\displaystyle\displaystyle C_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda}=\eta_{\mu\nu}\eta_{\rho\lambda}+\eta_{\mu\rho}\eta_{\nu\lambda}+\eta_{\mu\lambda}\eta_{\nu\rho}.

(241)

Lastly,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\ \ \int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}q_{\rho}q_{\lambda}q_{\delta}q_{\omega}}{(q^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{48}\bigg[\eta_{\mu\nu}\big(\eta_{\rho\lambda}\eta_{\delta\omega}+\eta_{\rho\delta}\eta_{\lambda\omega}+\eta_{\rho\omega}\eta_{\lambda\delta}\big)+\big(\nu\leftrightarrow\rho\big)+\big(\nu\leftrightarrow\lambda\big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\big(\nu\leftrightarrow\delta\big)+\big(\nu\leftrightarrow\omega\big)\bigg]\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{(q^{2})^{3}}{(q^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{i}{96\pi}F_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda\delta\omega}\bigg[\frac{\Lambda^{4}(\Lambda^{2}-3\alpha^{2}-\frac{6}{\Lambda^{2}}\alpha^{4})}{4(\Lambda^{2}+\alpha^{2})}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha^{4}\log\Big(\frac{\Lambda^{2}+\alpha^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)\bigg]\ ,

where,

\displaystyle\displaystyle F_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda\delta\omega}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\eta_{\mu\nu}\big(\eta_{\rho\lambda}\eta_{\delta\omega}+\eta_{\rho\delta}\eta_{\lambda\omega}+\eta_{\rho\omega}\eta_{\lambda\delta}\big)+\big(\nu\leftrightarrow\rho\big)+\big(\nu\leftrightarrow\lambda\big)+\big(\nu\leftrightarrow\delta\big)+\big(\nu\leftrightarrow\omega\big).

In the case where there is an odd number of momenta in the numerator, the integral vanishes as it is an odd function of the integration variable,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}q_{\mu_{i}}}{(q^{2}-\alpha^{2})^{2}}=

\displaystyle\displaystyle 0\ ,

(244)

where
\displaystyle n=\{0,2,4\}
.

The values of the above integrals, (
237
) - (
244
), will be used to derive useful results for evaluating the one-loop integrals that appear in section
4.1
.

\displaystyle s
-channel one-loop integrals

First, evaluate the one-loop integrals that appear while evaluating the
\displaystyle s
-channel amplitudes.
Evaluate the following finite integral,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\text{(i)}\ \ \ \ L^{(s)}(\xi)=\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{1}{\big[(\xi+q)^{2}-m^{2}\big]\big(q^{2}-m^{2}\big)}

(245)

where
\displaystyle\ \ \ \xi^{\mu}=(p_{1}+p_{2})^{\mu}
.
By introducing Feynman parametrization (
245
) can be written as,

\displaystyle\displaystyle L^{(s)}(\xi)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\ \frac{1}{\Big[(1-x)\big((\xi+q)^{2}-m^{2}\big)+x\big(q^{2}-m^{2}\big)\Big]^{2}}

(246)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dx\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\ \frac{1}{\Big[\big(q+\xi(1-x)\big)^{2}+\xi^{2}x(1-x)-m^{2}\Big]^{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dx\int\frac{d^{2}k}{(2\pi)^{2}}\ \frac{1}{\Big[k^{2}+\xi^{2}x(1-x)-m^{2}\Big]^{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{i}{4\pi}\int_{0}^{1}dx\frac{1}{\Big(m^{2}-\xi^{2}x(1-x)\Big)}\ ,

where in the third line the momentum was shifted
\displaystyle q\rightarrow k=q+\xi(1-x)
and in the fourth line (
237
) was used to evaluate the
\displaystyle k
integral.
Evaluating the
\displaystyle x
-integral yields,

\displaystyle\displaystyle L^{(s)}(\xi)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{i}{2\pi\xi^{2}\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}}\log\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}-1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}+1}\bigg).

(247)

It is useful to express the integral (
247
) in terms of the rapidity difference
\displaystyle\theta
. Recall that the Mandelstam variables
\displaystyle s=(p_{1}+p_{2})^{2}=\xi^{2}
,
\displaystyle t=0
and
\displaystyle u=4m^{2}-s
can be written in terms of
\displaystyle\theta
as,

\displaystyle\displaystyle s

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle 2m^{2}(1+\cosh\theta)=4m^{2}\cosh^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}

\displaystyle\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle 4m^{2}-s=-4m^{2}\sinh^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}.

(248)

Hence,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{s}}=\sqrt{-\frac{u}{s}}=\tanh\frac{\theta}{2}

(249)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\implies\log\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}-1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}+1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\log\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{-u/s}-1}{\sqrt{-u/s}+1}\bigg)=\log\bigg(\frac{\tanh\frac{\theta}{2}-1}{\tanh\frac{\theta}{2}+1}\bigg)=\log\big(-e^{-\theta}\big)=i\pi-\theta

where we used that
\displaystyle s
is really
\displaystyle s+i\epsilon
, in an attempt to pick the correct sheet.

Plugging everything into
\displaystyle L^{(s)}(\theta)
gives,

\displaystyle\displaystyle L^{(s)}(\theta)=-\frac{\pi+i\theta}{4\pi m^{2}\sinh\theta}.

(251)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\text{(ii)}\ \ \ \ L^{(s)}_{\mu}(\xi)=\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q_{\mu}}{\big[(\xi+q)^{2}-m^{2}\big]\big(q^{2}-m^{2}\big)}

(252)

Introduce the Feynman parameter
\displaystyle x
as before. Performing the
\displaystyle q
-integral and then the
\displaystyle x
-integral gives,

\displaystyle\displaystyle L^{(s)}_{\mu}(\xi)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{i\xi_{\mu}}{4\pi\xi^{2}\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}}\log\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}-1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}+1}\bigg).

(253)

Using (
249
) and (
) in (
253
) one obtains the final expression for
\displaystyle L^{(s)}_{\mu}(\theta)
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle L^{(s)}_{\mu}(\theta)=\xi_{\mu}\frac{\pi+i\theta}{8\pi m^{2}\sinh\theta}.

(254)

In a similar manner, using the Feynman parametrization one can derive the following integrals,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\text{(iii)}\ \ \ \ L^{(s)}_{\mu\nu}(\xi)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

(255)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{i}{8\pi}\bigg[1+\log\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}+\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}\log\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}-1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}+1}\bigg)\bigg]\eta_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{i}{4\pi\xi^{2}}\bigg[1+\frac{1-2\frac{m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}{\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}}\log\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}-1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{4m^{2}}{\xi^{2}}}+1}\bigg)\bigg]\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\nu}

\displaystyle\displaystyle-

(258)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-

(v)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\ L^{(s)}_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda\delta\omega}(\xi)=\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}q_{\rho}q_{\lambda}q_{\delta}q_{\omega}}{\big[(\xi+q)^{2}-m^{2}\big]\big(q^{2}-m^{2}\big)}

where,

\displaystyle\displaystyle H_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda\delta\omega}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle C_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda}\xi_{\delta}\xi_{\omega}+C_{\mu\nu\rho\delta}\xi_{\lambda}\xi_{\omega}+C_{\mu\nu\rho\omega}\xi_{\lambda}\xi_{\delta}+C_{\mu\nu\lambda\delta}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\omega}+C_{\mu\nu\lambda\omega}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\delta}+C_{\mu\rho\lambda\delta}\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\omega}

(266)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle C_{\mu\rho\lambda\omega}\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\delta}+C_{\nu\rho\lambda\delta}\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\omega}+C_{\nu\rho\lambda\omega}\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\delta}+C_{\mu\rho\delta\omega}\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\lambda}+C_{\mu\lambda\delta\omega}\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\rho}+C_{\lambda\rho\delta\omega}\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\mu}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle C_{\nu\rho\delta\omega}\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\lambda}+C_{\mu\nu\delta\omega}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\lambda}+C_{\nu\lambda\delta\omega}\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\rho}\ ,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\vskip 2.84526ptM_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda\delta\omega}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\lambda}\eta_{\delta\omega}+\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\delta}\eta_{\lambda\omega}+\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\omega}\eta_{\lambda\delta}+\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\lambda}\xi_{\delta}\eta_{\rho\omega}+\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\lambda}\xi_{\omega}\eta_{\rho\delta}

(267)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\lambda}\xi_{\delta}\eta_{\nu\omega}+\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\lambda}\xi_{\omega}\eta_{\nu\delta}+\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\lambda}\xi_{\delta}\eta_{\mu\omega}+\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\lambda}\xi_{\omega}\eta_{\mu\delta}+\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\delta}\xi_{\omega}\eta_{\nu\lambda}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\lambda}\xi_{\delta}\xi_{\omega}\eta_{\nu\rho}+\xi_{\lambda}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\delta}\xi_{\omega}\eta_{\nu\mu}+\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\rho}\xi_{\delta}\xi_{\omega}\eta_{\mu\lambda}+\xi_{\mu}\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\delta}\xi_{\omega}\eta_{\rho\lambda}+\xi_{\nu}\xi_{\lambda}\xi_{\delta}\xi_{\omega}\eta_{\mu\rho}

and
\displaystyle F_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda\delta\omega}

\displaystyle\displaystyle I^{(s)}_{abcd}(\xi)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}G_{ab}(\xi+q)G_{cd}(q)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{i\big(\gamma\cdot(\xi+q)+m\big)_{ab}}{(\xi+q)^{2}-m^{2}}\frac{i\big(\gamma\cdot q+m\big)_{cd}}{q^{2}-m^{2}}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{\gamma^{\mu}_{ab}\gamma^{\nu}_{cd}(\xi+q)_{\mu}q_{\nu}+m\delta_{ab}\gamma^{\mu}_{cd}q_{\mu}+m\gamma^{\mu}_{ab}(\xi+q)_{\mu}\delta_{cd}+m^{2}\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}}{\big[(\xi+q)^{2}-m^{2}\big](q^{2}-m^{2})}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\Big(\gamma^{\mu}_{ab}\gamma^{\nu}_{cd}\xi_{\mu}L^{(s)}_{\nu}(\xi)+\gamma^{\mu}_{ab}\gamma^{\nu}_{cd}L^{(s)}_{\mu\nu}(\xi)+m\delta_{ab}\gamma^{\mu}_{cd}L^{(s)}_{\mu}(\xi)+m\gamma^{\mu}_{ab}\xi_{\mu}\delta_{cd}L^{(s)}(\xi)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-L^{(s)}(\xi)\Big(m\gamma^{\mu}_{ab}\xi_{\mu}\delta_{cd}+m^{2}\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)-\Big(\gamma^{\mu}_{ab}\xi_{\mu}\gamma^{\nu}_{cd}+m\delta_{ab}\gamma^{\nu}_{cd}+m\gamma^{\nu}_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)L^{(s)}_{\nu}(\xi)

(269)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-L^{(s)}(\xi)\Big(m\not{\xi}_{ab}\delta_{cd}+m^{2}\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)-\Big(\not{\xi}_{ab}\not{L}^{(s)}_{cd}(\xi)+m\delta_{ab}\not{L}^{(s)}_{cd}(\xi)+m\not{L}^{(s)}_{ab}(\xi)\delta_{cd}\Big)

\displaystyle u
-channel one-loop integrals

Finally, evaluate the one-loop integrals that appear while evaluating the
\displaystyle u
-channel amplitudes. When there are no derivatives the one-loop integral can be written as,

\displaystyle\displaystyle I_{abcd}^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}G_{ab}(\zeta-q)G_{cd}(q)=\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{i\big(\gamma\cdot(\zeta-q)+m\big)_{ab}}{(\zeta-q)^{2}-m^{2}}\frac{i\big(\gamma\cdot q+m\big)_{cd}}{q^{2}-m^{2}}

(276)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{\gamma^{\mu}_{ab}\gamma^{\nu}_{cd}(\zeta-q)_{\mu}q_{\nu}+m\delta_{ab}\gamma^{\mu}_{cd}q_{\mu}+m\gamma^{\mu}_{ab}(\zeta-q)_{\mu}\delta_{cd}+m^{2}\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}}{\big[(\zeta-q)^{2}-m^{2}\big](q^{2}-m^{2})}

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-L^{(u)}(\zeta)\Big(m\not{\zeta}_{ab}\delta_{cd}+m^{2}\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)-\Big(\not{\zeta}_{ab}\not{L}_{cd}^{(u)}(\zeta)+m\delta_{ab}\not{L}_{cd}^{(u)}(\zeta)-m\not{L}_{ab}^{(u)}(\zeta)\delta_{cd}\Big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle+\gamma^{\mu}_{ab}\gamma^{\nu}_{cd}L_{\mu\nu}^{(u)}(\zeta)\ ,

where
\displaystyle\zeta_{\mu}=(p_{1}-p_{4})_{\mu}=(p_{1}-p_{2})_{\mu}
,
\displaystyle\not{\zeta}_{ab}=\gamma^{\mu}_{ab}\zeta_{\mu}
and

\displaystyle\displaystyle L^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{1}{\big[(\zeta-q)^{2}-m^{2}\big]\big(q^{2}-m^{2}\big)}\ ,

\displaystyle\displaystyle L_{\mu}^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q_{\mu}}{\big[(\zeta-q)^{2}-m^{2}\big]\big(q^{2}-m^{2}\big)}\ ,

\displaystyle\displaystyle L_{\mu\nu}^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}}{\big[(\zeta-q)^{2}-m^{2}\big]\big(q^{2}-m^{2}\big)}\ .

(277)

Comparing the above integrals with their
\displaystyle s
-channel counterparts (
245
,
252
and
255
) one sees that the structure of the integrals are identical apart from the fact that the
\displaystyle u
-channel integrands depends on
\displaystyle\zeta-q
while the
\displaystyle s
-channel integrands depend on
\displaystyle\xi+q
.

Since the
\displaystyle u
-channel corresponds to
\displaystyle\theta\rightarrow i\pi-\theta
, the above integrals can be derived directly from the results of their
\displaystyle s
-channel counterparts (

,
254
and
256

\displaystyle\theta\rightarrow i\pi-\theta
. Notice that replacing
\displaystyle\theta
by
\displaystyle i\pi-\theta
is equivalent to replacing
\displaystyle\xi^{2}
by
\displaystyle\zeta^{2}
. However, since the functional dependence of the integrands on
\displaystyle\zeta
is
\displaystyle\zeta-q
, an extra minus sign must be included whenever the integrand is odd in
\displaystyle q
. Thus,

\displaystyle\displaystyle L^{(u)}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle i\frac{\theta}{4\pi m^{2}\sinh\theta}

\displaystyle\displaystyle L^{(u)}_{\mu}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle i\frac{\theta}{8\pi m^{2}\sinh\theta}\zeta_{\mu}

\displaystyle\displaystyle L^{(u)}_{\mu\nu}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{i}{8\pi}\bigg[1+\log\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-\theta\coth\frac{\theta}{2}\bigg]\eta_{\mu\nu}-\frac{i}{16\pi m^{2}\sinh^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}}\big(1-\theta\coth\theta\big)\zeta_{\mu}\zeta_{\nu}

(278)

In a similar manner, one can derive the one-loop integrals in the
\displaystyle u
-channel case when there are derivatives,

\displaystyle\displaystyle(I_{\mu})_{abcd}^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\Big(m\not{\zeta}_{ab}\delta_{cd}+m^{2}\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)L_{\mu}^{(u)}(\zeta)-\Big(\not{\zeta}_{ab}\gamma^{\nu}_{cd}+m\delta_{ab}\gamma^{\nu}_{cd}+m\gamma^{\nu}_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)L_{\mu\nu}^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\gamma^{\nu}_{ab}\gamma^{\rho}_{cd}L_{\mu\nu\rho}^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}q_{\mu}q_{\nu}G_{ab}(\zeta-q)G_{cd}(q)

(280)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\Big(m\not{\zeta}_{ab}\delta_{cd}+m^{2}\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)L_{\mu\nu}^{(u)}(\zeta)-\Big(\not{\zeta}_{ab}\gamma^{\rho}_{cd}+m\delta_{ab}\gamma^{\rho}_{cd}+m\gamma^{\rho}_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)L_{\mu\nu\rho}^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}q_{\mu}q_{\nu}q_{\rho}G_{ab}(\zeta-q)G_{cd}(q)

(281)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\Big(m\not{\zeta}_{ab}\delta_{cd}+m^{2}\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)L_{\mu\nu\rho}^{(u)}(\zeta)-\Big(\not{\zeta}_{ab}\gamma^{\lambda}_{cd}+m\delta_{ab}\gamma^{\lambda}_{cd}+m\gamma^{\lambda}_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)L_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda}^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\gamma^{\lambda}_{ab}\gamma^{\delta}_{cd}L_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda\delta}^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle(I_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda})_{abcd}^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}q_{\mu}q_{\nu}q_{\rho}q_{\lambda}G_{ab}(\zeta-q)G_{cd}(q)

(282)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\Big(m\not{\zeta}_{ab}\delta_{cd}+m^{2}\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)L_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda}^{(u)}(\zeta)-\Big(\not{\zeta}_{ab}\gamma^{\delta}_{cd}+m\delta_{ab}\gamma^{\delta}_{cd}+m\gamma^{\delta}_{ab}\delta_{cd}\Big)L_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda\delta}^{(u)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\gamma^{\delta}_{ab}\gamma^{\omega}_{cd}L_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda\delta\omega}^{(u)}(\zeta)\ ,

where,

\displaystyle\displaystyle L_{\mu\nu\rho}^{(u)}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}q_{\rho}}{\big[(\zeta-q)^{2}-m^{2}\big]\big(q^{2}-m^{2}\big)}

(283)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{i}{16\pi}\big(1+\log\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-\theta\coth\frac{\theta}{2}\big)\big(\eta_{\mu\nu}\zeta_{\rho}+\eta_{\nu\rho}\zeta_{\mu}+\eta_{\rho\mu}\zeta_{\nu}\big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{i}{32\pi m^{2}\sinh^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}}\big(3-2\theta\coth\theta-\theta\csch\theta\big)\zeta_{\mu}\zeta_{\nu}\zeta_{\rho}

\displaystyle\displaystyle L_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda}^{(u)}(\theta)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}q}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}q_{\rho}q_{\lambda}}{\big[(\zeta-q)^{2}-m^{2}\big]\big(q^{2}-m^{2}\big)}

(284)

\displaystyle\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle-\frac{im^{2}}{288\pi}\bigg[14-9\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}+12\log\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}+2\cosh\theta\big(5+3\log\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}\big)-6\theta\big(2\coth\frac{\theta}{2}+\sinh\theta\big)\bigg]C_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{i}{288\pi\sinh^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}}\bigg[1+6\log\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}-\cosh\theta\big(7+6\log\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{m^{2}}\big)+3\theta\cosh\frac{3\theta}{2}\csch\frac{\theta}{2}\bigg]\bigg[\eta_{\mu\nu}\zeta_{\rho}\zeta_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\displaystyle+
